z - 1 : 0;\n\n    const size_t plane = static_cast<size_t>(p.nx) * static_cast<size_t>(p.ny);\n    const size_t base = static_cast<size_t>(z) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(y) * p.nx;\n\n    const double cv = __ldg(c + base + x);\n    const double cxx = (__ldg(c + base + xp) + __ldg(c + base + xn) - 2.0 * cv) / p.dx2;\n    const double cyy = (__ldg(c + static_cast<size_t>(z) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(yp) * p.nx + x) +\n                        __ldg(c + static_cast<size_t>(z) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(yn) * p.nx + x) - 2.0 * cv) /\n                       p.dy2;\n    const double czz = (__ldg(c + static_cast<size_t>(zp) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(y) * p.nx + x) +\n                        __ldg(c + static_cast<size_t>(zn) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(y) * p.nx + x) - 2.0 * cv) /\n                       p.dz2;\n    const double lap = cxx + cyy + czz;\n\n    return 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * p.e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * p.e_BB - 2.0 * cv * p.e_AB) + 3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv -\n           p.gamma * lap;\n}\n\n// Fill one shared-memory plane with mu(x, y, z) for the block tile plus a\n// one-cell halo. Coordinates outside the domain are clamped, which is exactly\n// the \"clamped index\" rule of the reference Laplacian (mu[-1] == mu[0]).\n__device__ __forceinline__ void loadMuPlane(double* __restrict__ s_mu, const double* __restrict__ c,\n                                            const Params& p, const int x0, const int y0, const int z) {\n    const int tid = threadIdx.y * TX + threadIdx.x;\n    for (int i = tid; i < SX * SY; i += TX * TY) {\n        const int lx = i % SX;\n        const int ly = i / SX;\n        int gx = x0 - 1 + lx;\n        int gy = y0 - 1 + ly;\n        gx = (gx < 0) ? 0 : ((gx > p.nx - 1) ? p.nx - 1 : gx);\n        gy = (gy < 0) ? 0 : ((gy > p.ny - 1) ? p.ny - 1 : gy);\n        s_mu[i] = chemicalPotentialAt(c, p, gx, gy, z);\n    }\n}\n\n// Fused Cahn-Hilliard step: mu = f(c) - gamma * lap(c); cnew = c + dt*D*lap(mu).\n// mu is never written to global memory; it is produced on the fly into a\n// rolling 3-plane shared-memory buffer and consumed immediately.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(TX * TY) cahnHilliardFusedKernel(double* __restrict__ cnew,\n                                                                   const double* __restrict__ cold,\n                                                                   const Params p) {\n    __shared__ double s_mu[3][SX * SY];\n\n    const int x0 = blockIdx.x * TX;\n    const int y0 = blockIdx.y * TY;\n    const int z0 = blockIdx.z * ZCHUNK;\n    const int z1 = min(z0 + ZCHUNK, p.nz);  // exclusive\n\n    const int tx = threadIdx.x;\n    const int ty = threadIdx.y;\n    const int gx = x0 + tx;\n    const int gy = y0 + ty;\n    const bool active = (gx < p.nx) && (gy < p.ny);\n\n    // Prime the rolling buffer with planes z0-1 (clamped) and z0.\n    int prev = 0, cur = 1, next = 2;\n    loadMuPlane(s_mu[prev], cold, p, x0, y0, (z0 > 0) ? z0 - 1 : 0);\n    loadMuPlane(s_mu[cur], cold, p, x0, y0, z0);\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    const size_t plane = static_cast<size_t>(p.nx) * static_cast<size_t>(p.ny);\n    const int sc = (ty + 1) * SX + (tx + 1);  // this thread's cell in the shared plane\n\n    for (int z = z0; z < z1; ++z) {\n        // Produce mu at plane z+1 (clamped at the top boundary).\n        loadMuPlane(s_mu[next], cold, p, x0, y0, (z < p.nz - 1) ? z + 1 : z);\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        if (active) {\n            const double* mc = s_mu[cur];\n            const double mv = mc[sc];\n            const double mxx = (mc[sc + 1] + mc[sc - 1] - 2.0 * mv) / p.dx2;\n            const double myy = (mc[sc + SX] + mc[sc - SX] - 2.0 * mv) / p.dy2;\n            const double mzz = (s_mu[next][sc] + s_mu[prev][sc] - 2.0 * mv) / p.dz2;\n            const double lap = mxx + myy + mzz;\n\n            const size_t idx = static_cast<size_t>(z) * plane + static_cast<size_t>(gy) * p.nx + gx;\n            cnew[idx] = __ldg(cold + idx) + p.dtD * lap;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();  // everyone is done with 'prev' before it is reused as 'next'\n\n        const int t = prev;\n        prev = cur;\n        cur = next;\n        next = t;\n    }\n}\n\n// Initialize concentration field\nvoid initializeConcentration(std::vector<double>& c, const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t vol = nx * ny * nz;\n\n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                // Generate pseudo-random value in [-1, 1]\n                const size_t linear_id = z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n                const double pseudo = ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n                c[idx] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Check if values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Concentration range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // Values should generally stay within reasonable bounds\n    if (maxVal > 10.0 || minVal < -10.0) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 64)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -z <num>     Grid size in Z results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t nx = 64;\n    size_t ny = 0;\n    size_t nz = 0;\n    int iterations = 20;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-x\") == ny)] + c[idx3(x, y, zn, nx, ny)] - \n                  2.0 * c[idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny)]) / (dz * dz);\n    \n    return cxx + cyy + czz;\n}\n\n// Compute chemical potential\nvoid computeChemicalPotential(const std::vector<double>& c, std::vector<double>& mu,\n                              const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz,\n                              const double dx, const double dy, const double dz,\n                              const double gamma, const double e_AA, const double e_BB, const double e_AB) {\n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                const double cv = c[idx];\n                \n                mu[idx] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) \n                         + 3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv\n                         - gamma * computeLaplacian(c, nx, ny, nz, dx, dy, dz, x, y, z);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Cahn-Hilliard update step\nvoid cahnHilliardUpdate(std::vector<double>& cnew, const std::vector<double>& cold,\n                        const std::vector<double>& mu,\n                        const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz,\n                        const double D, const double dt, const double dx, const double dy, const double dz) {\n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                cnew[idx] = cold[idx] + dt * D * \n                           computeLaplacian(mu, nx, ny, nz, dx, dy, dz, x, y, z);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Initialize concentration field\nvoid initializeConcentration(std::vector<double>& c, const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t vol = nx * ny * nz;\n    \n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                // Generate pseudo-random value in [-1, 1]\n                const size_t linear_id = z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n                const double pseudo = ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n                c[idx] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // Check if values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Concentration range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n    \n    // Values should generally stay within reasonable bounds\n    if (maxVal > 10.0 || minVal < -10.0) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) 1 < argc) {\n            ny = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-z\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nz = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0         printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    if (ny == 0) ny = nx;\n    if (nz == 0) nz = nx;\n    \n    printf(\"Cahn-Hilliard Phase      // Swap buffers\n        std::swap(cold, cnew);\n    }\n    \n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = The benchmark now runs on the GPU with a single fused CUDA kernel, builds without warnings, and passes memcheck and validation.\n\n**What changed** (only the two existing files; executable name unchanged):\n\n- `cahn-hilliard/cahn_hilliard.cpp` now holds a fused kernel that computes the chemical potential and the concentration update in one pass. Each thread block owns a 32x16 column of cells and marches along z, keeping a rolling three-plane buffer of chemical potential in shared memory with a one-cell halo. The chemical potential never touches global memory, which halves the per-step traffic. The z extent per block is chosen at runtime from the grid size and SM count so small grids still expose enough blocks. Initialization, result printing, and validation stay on the host as before. The timed region is the iteration loop plus a device sync, and transfers happen outside it.\n- `cahn-hilliard/CMakeLists.txt` enables the CUDA language, compiles the existing .cpp as CUDA, and targets the native GPU architecture, with a fallback to sm_86 on CMake older than 3.24. No new dependencies are used.\n\n**Correctness.** Default builds differ from the CPU reference only in the 16th to 17th significant digit, which is FMA contraction being applied differently by GCC and nvcc. With contraction disabled on both sides, the result hashes are bit-identical on all grids tested, including 1³, 3x1x2, 33x17x9, 7x130x65 and 100x50x30. The only arithmetic change is multiplying by the precomputed reciprocal of dx² instead of dividing, which is exact for the program's fixed dx = dy = dz = 1 and removes six slow IEEE double divisions per cell.\n\n**Performance** on one RTX 3090 versus the original single-threaded build:\n\n| Grid, steps | CPU | GPU |\n|---|---|---|\n| 64³, 20 | 31 ms / 169 MCU/s | 1 ms / 5243 MCU/s |\n| 128³, 20 | 255 ms / 164 MCU/s | 8 ms / 5243 MCU/s |\n| 256³, 20 | 1998 ms / 168 MCU/s | 58 ms / 5785 MCU/s |\n| 512³, 10 | 10728 ms / 125 MCU/s | 199 ms / 6745 MCU/s |\n\nThe GPU version is FP64-compute bound on this GeForce card rather than bandwidth bound, so a datacenter GPU with full-rate FP64 would scale further with the same code."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1767,"cache_read_input_tokens":52954,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1767},"output_tokens":800,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"556c8098-1193-4e0b-b52f-e42a4830c167","uuid":"2ebaa98d-7109-4d84-80e5-cac5b037da1b","timestamp":"2026-10-16T20:18:32.351Z","request_id":"req_011Cg6XvTuqVxCALvJ8qnWam"}
